breaks the gauge group
G
to a Levi subgroup
\mathbb{L}\subseteq G
, which in applications to
\widehat{Z}
-invariants is a maximal torus of
G
. In particular,
\mathbb{L}=\operatorname{U}(1)
for
G=\operatorname{SU}(2)
. Other choices of
\mathbb{L}
are also interesting, and lead to a generalization of
\widehat{Z}
-invariants labeled by complex coadjoint orbits of
G_{\mathbb{C}}
or, equivalently, by
\rho\colon\mathfrak{sl}(2)\to\mathfrak{g}

[
45
]
.

When
\mathbb{L}=\mathbb{T}
, there are infinitely many different topological sectors labeled by monopole numbers
b_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in\Lambda_{\text{cochar}}=\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{U}(1),\mathbb{T})
or, more precisely, by spin
c
structures. In order to keep track of dependence on
b
, one can introduce a topological term
\exp(2\pi{\rm i}\eta b)
in the action of 4d gauge theory. Then, using Pontryagin duality,

x={\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\eta}\in\operatorname{Hom}(H_{1}(M),\mathbb{T})

can be identified with the variable by the same name in
F_{K}(x,q)
and in the integrand of
\widehat{Z}
-invariants. To summarize, on a closed 3-manifold with
b_{1}>0
the boundary condition
{\mathcal{B}}_{b}
breaks the gauge group
G
to
\mathbb{L}=\mathbb{T}
and creates a “flux”
b
. When this flux is non-torsion, the solutions to Kapustin–Witten PDE’s on
M\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
can not approach a constant field configuration at
y=+\infty
. At best one can require solutions to approach a field configuration periodic in the
y
-direction, which leads us to conclude that the anomaly in question is controlled by the moduli space of solutions on
M\times S^{1}
with gauge group
\mathbb{L}
. Below we give another interpretation of this claim from the perspective of 3d-3d correspondence.

From the point of view of 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
theory
T[M]
, the boundary condition
{\mathcal{B}}_{b}
labels the background momentum / charge sectors of the 2d boundary theory, cf.
[
29
]
. In the partition function on
D^{2}\times_{q}S^{1}
, the parameter
q
keeps track of the spin with respect to the rotation symmetry of
D^{2}
. It is defined up to spins of BPS states in 2d boundary theory or, equivalently, 3d theory
T[M,\mathbb{T}]
on a slab
T^{2}\times I
with boundary conditions
{\mathcal{B}}_{b}
on both sides. This theory is a close cousin of
T\big[M\times S^{1},\mathbb{T}\big]
in the background of spin
c
structure
b
on
M
. Indeed, both theories exhibit a qualitative change in behavior depending on whether
b_{1}=0
or
b_{1}>0
. In the latter case, the BPS states come in towers infinite in both direction, with spins in each tower being multiples of
b^{\prime\prime}
, which leads again to the ambiguity (
2.35
).

Similarly, from the curve counting perspective on
T^{*}M
, when
b_{1}>0
in addition to open BPS states one also has a non-trivial “closed sector.”

3
Families of examples

In this section, we illustrate the proposed relation with a number of instructive examples for which explicit formulas for
\widehat{Z}
can be provided (recall Remark
2.16
).

3.1
Plumbed 3-manifolds

Here we consider the case when
M
is a rational homology sphere given by a weakly negative definite plumbing graph
\Gamma

[
40
,
43
]
. We can then assume that

\omega\in H^{1}(M;\mathbb{Q}/2\mathbb{Z})\setminus H^{1}(M;\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}).

With the notation of Section
2.2
, formula (
2.26
) becomes

\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)=\frac{1}{\Delta_{+}^{b_{+}}\Delta_{-}^{b_{-}}}\sum_{k\in H_{r}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\prod_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}d(\alpha_{k_{I}})^{2-\deg(I)}T(\alpha_{k_{I}})^{B_{II}}\prod_{(I,J)\in\text{Edges}}S(\alpha_{k_{I}},\alpha_{k_{J}}),

where

\alpha_{k_{I}}:=\mu_{I}+k_{I}

and
b_{\pm}
are the number of positive/negative eigenvalues of
B
. In this section, we will be somewhat cavalier with taking the limits and about convergence of infinite series. Such technical details will be properly addressed in Section
4.1
and Appendix
D
.

It is instructive to separate 3 factors:

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)=\mathcal{A}\cdot\mathcal{B}\cdot\mathcal{C},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=r^{-V/2}\xi^{\frac{3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}\cdot\begin{cases}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}(\sigma+\operatorname{Tr}B)}{2}},&r=1\bmod 4,\\
2^{-V/2}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}},&r=2\bmod 4,\\
{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}\operatorname{Tr}B}(-1)^{\sigma},&r=3\bmod 4,\end{cases}

where
\sigma
is the signature of
B
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}=F\big(\big\{{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}\big\}_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}\big)^{-1},

(3.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}=\sum_{k\in H_{r}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}F\big(\big\{\xi^{\mu_{I}+k_{I}}\big\}_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}\big)\cdot\xi^{\frac{1}{2}(\mu+k)^{T}B(\mu+k)},

(3.2)

and

F(x):=\prod_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}(x_{I}-1/x_{I})^{2-\deg(I)}=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}F_{\ell}\prod_{I}x_{I}^{\ell_{I}}

(3.3)

is a slightly different normalization of the invariant
F_{L}(x,q)
for the case of the plumbing link
L
. Consider a contribution of a monomial
\prod_{I}x_{I}^{\ell_{I}}
from
F(x)
into (
3.2
):

\mathcal{C}_{\ell}:=\sum_{k\in H_{r}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\xi^{\ell^{T}(\mu+k)}\cdot\xi^{\frac{1}{2}(\mu+k)^{T}B(\mu+k)}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/r\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2r}(\tilde{\mu}+2n)^{T}B(\tilde{\mu}+2n)+\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{r}\ell^{T}(\tilde{\mu}+2n)},

(3.4)

where

\tilde{\mu}:=\mu+(r-1)\varepsilon

and
\varepsilon
is the vector
(1,1,\ldots,1,1)
.
We can now use the following version of Gauss reciprocity formula
[
31
,
48
]
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/r\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\exp\bigg(\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}n^{T}Bn+\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}p^{T}n\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}(r/2)^{V/2}}{|\det{B}|^{1/2}}\sum_{\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\exp\biggl(-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}\bigg(\tilde{a}+\frac{p}{r}\bigg)^{T}B^{-1}\bigg(\tilde{a}+\frac{p}{r}\bigg)\biggr).

(3.5)

Applying it to (
3.4
), we have

\mathcal{C}_{\ell}=\xi^{-\frac{\ell^{T}B^{-1}\ell}{2}}\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}(r/2)^{V/2}}{|\det B|^{1/2}}\underbrace{\sum_{\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}\tilde{a}^{T}B^{-1}\tilde{a}-\pi{\rm i}\tilde{a}^{T}B^{-1}(\ell+B\tilde{\mu})}}_{=:\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\prime}}.

(3.6)

Let us make the change of variables
\tilde{a}=BA+a
,
A\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}
,
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\sum_{A\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}{a}^{T}B^{-1}{a}-\pi{\rm i}rA^{T}a-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}A^{T}BA-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}(\ell+B\tilde{\mu})-\pi{\rm i}A^{T}(\ell+B\tilde{\mu})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\sum_{A\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\exp\biggl\{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}{a}^{T}B^{-1}{a}-\pi{\rm i}rA^{T}a-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}A^{T}BA

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}(s+\mu-r\varepsilon)-\pi{\rm i}A^{T}B(s-r\varepsilon)\biggr\},

(3.7)

where in the last line we used the fact that (
3.3
) only contains powers
\prod_{I}x_{I}^{\ell_{I}}
satisfying
\ell_{I}=\deg(I)\bmod 2
, and therefore one can introduce
b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}
,
s\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}
,
\sum_{J}B_{IJ}s_{J}=B_{II}\bmod 2
, such that

\ell=2b+B(s-\varepsilon)\bmod 2B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}.

(3.8)

We also used the property (
2.22
). At this point we will need to consider the cases with different
r\bmod 4
values separately.

3.2
Level
\bm{r=2\bmod 4}

Using the fact that
r
is even, while
r/2
is an odd integer, and condition on
s
the sum (
3.7
) simplifies to

\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\prime}=2^{V}\sum_{{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\exp\biggl\{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}{a}^{T}B^{-1}{a}-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}(s+\mu)\biggr\}.

Combining everything together we then have

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)={}

\displaystyle\frac{F\big(\big\{{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}\big\}_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}\big)^{-1}}{|\det B|^{1/2}}\xi^{\frac{3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}

\displaystyle{}\times\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\sum_{{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}F_{\ell}\xi^{-\frac{\ell^{T}B^{-1}\ell}{2}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}{a}^{T}B^{-1}{a}-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}(s+\mu)}.

Using the following expressions for
\widehat{Z}
and Reidemeister torsion
14
14

14

In principle, the torsion
\mathcal{T}(M,\alpha)
, defined for
\alpha\in H^{1}(M;\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\ell k}}}{{\cong}}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})
, has sign ambiguity, if no additional structures on
M
are introduced. One can fix the sign for example by introducing a spin structure
s\in\mathrm{Spin}(M)
on
M
, cf.
[
59
]
. The change of the spin structure
s\rightarrow s+c
,
c\in H^{2}(M;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
then changes the sign by
(-1)^{c(\tilde{\alpha})}
where
\tilde{\alpha}\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})
is dual to
\alpha
. However, since in our case
\alpha=\omega\bmod 1
,
\tilde{\alpha}
is even, and the dependence on spin structure drops out.

\mathcal{T}
for plumbed manifolds (see formula (
A.1
) in Appendix
A
)

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{a}(M)=(-1)^{b_{+}}q^{\frac{3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B}{4}}\sum_{\ell=a\bmod 2B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}F_{\ell}q^{-\frac{\ell^{T}B^{-1}\ell}{4}},\qquad a_{I}=\deg(I)\bmod 2,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])=(-1)^{b_{+}}\prod_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}\big({\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}-{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}\big)^{\deg(I)-2},\qquad[\omega]:=\omega\bmod H^{1}(M;\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})

(3.9)

and the identifications (
2.2
)–(
2.18
), we can conjecture the following general relation for a rational homology sphere
M
and
r=2\bmod 4
:

\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)=\frac{\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])}{\sqrt{|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}}\sum_{a,b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(a)-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(a,b)-\pi{\rm i}\omega(a)}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b,s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}},

(3.10)

where
\sigma
is the canonical map

\sigma\colon\ H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\times\operatorname{Spin}(M)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Spin}^{c}(M)

(3.11)

producing a spin
c
structure on
M
from a spin structure
c
and
\tilde{b}\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})
. It is induced by the map
B\operatorname{Spin}\times B\operatorname{U}(1)\rightarrow B\operatorname{Spin}^{c}
between the corresponding classifying spaces, combined with the isomorphisms
B\operatorname{U}(1)\cong B^{2}\mathbb{Z}
,
H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\cong H^{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
. In (
3.10
), we have introduced an auxiliary spin structure
s\in\mathrm{Spin}(M)
(see also formula (
3.8
)). The result is independent of it due to (
2.14
), so that the simultaneous change of
b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})
and
s\in\mathrm{Spin}(M)
leaving
\sigma(b,s)
invariant also leaves invariant the exponent in the sum in (
3.10
).

3.3
Level
\bm{r=1\bmod 4}

The sum (
3.7
) reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\prime}=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\sum_{A\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\exp\biggl\{-

\displaystyle\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}a^{T}B^{-1}a-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}\mu

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\pi{\rm i}a^{T}(s-\varepsilon)-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}A^{T}BA+\pi{\rm i}A^{T}(a+B(s-\varepsilon))\biggr\}.

Applying a version of the Gauss reciprocity formula to the sum over
A
, we can rewrite it as follows:

\begin{split}\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\prime}=\frac{{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}2^{V/2}}{|\det B|^{1/2}}\sum_{a,f\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\exp\biggl\{{-}&\frac{\pi{\rm i}(r-1)}{2}a^{T}B^{-1}a-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}\mu\\
+&2\pi{\rm i}f^{T}B^{-1}f+2\pi{\rm i}f^{T}B^{-1}a+\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}(s-\varepsilon)^{T}B(s-\varepsilon)\biggr\}.\end{split}

Taking into account that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{T}B\varepsilon=\frac{V-1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{Tr}B\bmod 1,\qquad\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{T}Bs=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{Tr}B\bmod 1,

and combining everything together we then have

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)={}

\displaystyle\frac{F(\{{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}\}_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}})^{-1}}{|\det B|}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}(2-\sigma+s^{T}Bs)}\xi^{\frac{3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\sum_{a,f\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}F_{\ell}\xi^{-\frac{\ell^{T}B^{-1}\ell}{2}}

\displaystyle{}\times{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}(r-1)}{2}a^{T}B^{-1}a-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}\mu+2\pi{\rm i}f^{T}B^{-1}f+2\pi{\rm i}f^{T}B^{-1}a}.

As in the case
r=2\bmod 4
, we can then conjecture the following general relation for a rational homology sphere
M
and
r=1\bmod 4
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)={}

\displaystyle\frac{-{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}\mu(M,s)}\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])}{{|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}}

\displaystyle\times{}\sum_{a,b,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r-1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)+{\ell k}(a,f-b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)+{\ell k}(f,f)\right)}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b,s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}},

(3.12)

where we have used the surgery formula (
2.17
) for the
\pmod{4}
reduction of the Rokhlin invariant
\mu(M,s)
.

It is interesting to remark that unlike in the case
r=2\pmod{4}
here the relation between
\mathrm{N}_{r}
and
\widehat{Z}
is based on a
triple
summation (instead of double).

3.4
Level
\bm{r=3\bmod 4}

This case is analogous to the case
r=1\bmod 4
considered above. When
r=3\bmod 4
, the sum (
3.7
) reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\prime}=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\sum_{A\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\exp\biggl\{-

\displaystyle\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}a^{T}B^{-1}a-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}\mu

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\pi{\rm i}a^{T}(s-\varepsilon)+\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}A^{T}BA+\pi{\rm i}A^{T}(a+B(s-\varepsilon))\biggr\}.

Applying again the Gauss reciprocity formula to the sum over
A
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\prime}=\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}2^{V/2}}{|\det B|^{1/2}}\sum_{a,f\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\exp\biggl\{-

\displaystyle\frac{\pi{\rm i}(r+1)}{2}a^{T}B^{-1}a-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}\mu

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\pi{\rm i}f^{T}B^{-1}f-2\pi{\rm i}f^{T}B^{-1}a-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}(s-\varepsilon)^{T}B(s-\varepsilon)\biggr\}.

Combining everything together we then have

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)={}

\displaystyle\frac{F(\{{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}\}_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}})^{-1}}{|\det B|}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}(2+\sigma-s^{T}B^{-1}s)}\xi^{\frac{3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\sum_{a,f\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}F_{\ell}\xi^{-\frac{\ell^{T}B^{-1}\ell}{2}}

\displaystyle\times{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}(r-1)}{2}a^{T}B^{-1}a-2\pi{\rm i}a^{T}B^{-1}b-\pi{\rm i}a^{T}\mu+2\pi{\rm i}f^{T}B^{-1}f+2\pi{\rm i}f^{T}B^{-1}a}.

We can then conjecture the following general relation for a rational homology sphere
M
and
r=3\bmod 4
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)=

\displaystyle\frac{-{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}\mu(M,s)}\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])}{{|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}}

(3.13)

\displaystyle\times{}\sum_{a,b,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r+1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)-{\ell k}(a,f+b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)-{\ell k}(f,f)\right)}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b,s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}.

3.5
Generalization to
\bm{b_{1}>0}

Let
M
be, as before, obtained by a surgery on a link with the linking matrix
B
. However, now we will allow
B
to be degenerate. By an
{\rm SL}(V,\mathbb{Z})
transform we can bring the quadratic form
B
to
B^{\prime}\oplus\mathbf{0}_{b_{1}}
where
\mathbf{0}_{b_{1}}
is the trivial quadratic form on
\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{V}

[
56
]
. The expression (
3.6
) then will be modified to

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\ell}={}

\displaystyle\xi^{-\frac{{\ell^{\prime}}^{T}{B^{\prime}}^{-1}{\ell^{\prime}}}{2}}\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}(r/2)^{(V-b_{1})/2}}{|\det B^{\prime}|^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\times{}\sum_{\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{V-b_{1}}/2B^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}^{V-b_{1}}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}\tilde{a}^{T}(B^{\prime})^{-1}\tilde{a}-\pi{\rm i}\tilde{a}^{T}(B^{\prime})^{-1}(\ell^{\prime}+B^{\prime}\tilde{\mu}^{\prime})}r^{b_{1}}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{T}\tilde{\mu}^{\prime\prime}}{r}}\delta_{\ell^{\prime\prime}=0\bmod r},

where
\ell=\ell^{\prime}\oplus\ell^{\prime\prime}
and
\tilde{\mu}=\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}\oplus\tilde{\mu}^{\prime\prime}
according to the splitting of
B
above.

Consider first the case of
r=2\bmod 4
. We have

\mathcal{A}=\xi^{\frac{3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}r^{-V/2+b_{1}/2}2^{b_{1}/2-V/2}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}.

The relation (
3.10
) generalizes to

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)

\displaystyle{}=\frac{r^{b_{1}}\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])}{\sqrt{|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(a^{\prime})-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})-\pi{\rm i}\omega(a^{\prime})+\pi{\rm i}\omega^{\prime\prime}(m)}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b^{\prime}\oplus rm/2,s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}

\displaystyle{}=\frac{r^{b_{1}}\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])}{\sqrt{|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}}\int_{H^{1}(M;\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z})}\!\!\!\!\mu(\alpha)\!\!\!\!\sum_{b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(\alpha^{\prime})-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(\alpha^{\prime},b^{\prime})-\pi{\rm i}\omega(\alpha^{\prime})+2\pi{\rm i}\alpha^{\prime\prime}(b^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt{}\times\delta(r\alpha^{\prime\prime}-\omega^{\prime\prime}/2)\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b,s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}},

(3.14)

where we chose explicit splittings
\omega=\omega^{\prime}\oplus\omega^{\prime\prime}\in H^{1}(M;\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})\cong\operatorname{Tor}{H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}\oplus(\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})^{b_{1}}
and
b=b^{\prime}\oplus b^{\prime\prime}\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\cong\operatorname{Tor}{H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}}
according to the splitting of the linking matrix
B
above.
It is straightforward to see that the right-hand side is independent of the choice of representative of the equivalence class (
2.35
). The coefficients in the relation (
3.14
) are
not
invariant under the automorphisms (
2.37
). However, this compensated by the non-covariance of
\widehat{Z}
. Taking into account (
2.40
), one can show that the total sum in (
3.14
) transforms covariantly (i.e., as a function on
H^{1}(M;\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})\cong\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}},\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})
).
Namely, considering that

q^{\mathcal{E}(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})}\big|_{b^{\prime\prime}=\frac{mr}{2}}={\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(\mu m)-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(\mu m,b^{\prime})}

and shifting the summation variables
b^{\prime}\rightarrow b^{\prime}+\frac{r}{2}\mu m
,
a^{\prime}\rightarrow a^{\prime}-\mu m
, we have indeed

\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega^{\prime}\oplus\omega^{\prime\prime})\rightsquigarrow\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega^{\prime}\oplus(\omega^{\prime\prime}+2\mu^{*}\omega)),

where

\omega^{\prime}\oplus\omega^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\oplus(\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})^{b_{1}}

and

\mu^{*}\colon\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow(\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{Z})^{b_{1}}

is the map dual to
\mu
in (
2.38
).

For
r=1\bmod 4
, we have

\mathcal{A}=r^{-V/2+b_{1}/2}\xi^{\frac{3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}(\operatorname{Tr}B+\sigma)}

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{b_{1}}\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])}{{|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a^{\prime},b^{\prime},f^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}}\end{subarray}}\exp\bigg\{2\pi{\rm i}\biggl(-\frac{r-1}{4}{\ell k}(a^{\prime},a^{\prime})+{\ell k}(a^{\prime},f^{\prime}-b^{\prime})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a^{\prime})+{\ell k}(f^{\prime},f^{\prime})-\Delta_{\sigma(b^{\prime},s)}-{\ell k}(b^{\prime},b^{\prime})+\omega^{\prime\prime}(m)\biggr)\bigg\}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b^{\prime}\oplus rm,s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}.

(3.15)

Example 3.1
.

An interesting example is
M=\Sigma_{g}\times S^{1}
.
In this case
{\ell k}=0
and
q_{s}=0
.
Furthermore, we have the identity:

\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])=(-2)^{b_{1}+1}\bigg(\frac{\rm i}{4}\bigg)^{b_{1}}\frac{\rm i}{2}\mathrm{N}_{2}(M,2\omega).

Thus replacing in the above formula we get

\frac{\mathrm{N}_{r}\big(\Sigma_{g}\times S^{1},\omega\big)}{\mathrm{N}_{2}\big(\Sigma_{g}\times S^{1},2\omega\big)}={r^{b_{1}}(-2)^{1+b_{1}}\bigg(\frac{\rm i}{4}\bigg)^{b_{1}}\frac{\rm i}{2}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2g+1}}{\rm e}^{+\pi{\rm i}\omega(m)}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(\frac{rm}{2},s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}.

If we now recall that

\mathrm{N}_{r}\big(\Sigma_{g}\times S^{1},\omega\big)=r^{2g}\sum_{k\in H_{r}}\bigg(\frac{\{r\beta\}}{\{\beta+k\}}\bigg)^{2g-2}

(
so in particular,

\mathrm{N}_{2}\big(\Sigma_{g}\times S^{1},2\omega\big)=2^{2g+1}\frac{1}{{\rm i}^{2g-2}}\big({\rm i}^{2\beta}-{\rm i}^{-2\beta}\big)^{2g-2}
)
, where
\beta=\omega\big(S^{1}\big)
(note the formula does not depend on the orientation of
S^{1}
), then we get

\frac{1}{r}\sum_{k\in H_{r}}\frac{\{r\beta\}^{2g-2}}{(\{\beta+k\}({\rm i}^{2\beta}-{\rm i}^{-2\beta}))^{2g-2}}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{2g+1}}{\rm e}^{+\pi{\rm i}\omega(m)}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(\frac{rm}{2},s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}.

(3.16)

In order to compute
\widehat{Z}_{\mathfrak{s}}
, we observe that the formula (
3.9
) providing the value of
\widehat{Z}
for surgeries over plumbing links can be generalised to manifolds which are the boundaries of a plumbing of surfaces in a tree-like fashion. In particular, for
M=\Sigma_{g}\times S^{1}
,

\big(x-x^{-1}\big)^{2-2g}=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{\ell}x^{\ell}.

(3.17)

Since
\operatorname{Tor}(H_{1}(M))=0
, the first Chern class provides a bijection between the set of spin
c
structures on
M
and
H^{2}(M)=H_{1}(M)
, but
\widehat{Z}_{\mathfrak{s}}
is zero for all spin
c
structures
\mathfrak{s}
such that
c_{1}(\mathfrak{s})\neq PD\big(\ell\big[\{pt\}\times S^{1}\big]\big)
for some
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
. So letting
\ell
be the spin
c
structure the first Chern class of which is Poincaré dual to
\ell\big[\{pt\}\times S^{1}\big]
and using (
2.33
) with
U={\rm Id}
,
B=0
,
\sigma=0=b_{+}
, we have

\widehat{Z}_{\ell}=q^{\ell}F_{\ell}
.

Therefore, equation (
3.16
) becomes

\sum_{k\in H_{r}}\frac{1}{\big({\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{r}(\beta+k)}-{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{r}(\beta+k)}\big)^{2g-2}}=r\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}m\beta}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(\frac{rm}{2},s)}\big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}.

(3.18)

Then we have that the left-hand side of (
3.18
) equals

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{\ell}\sum_{k\in H_{r}}{\rm e}^{\ell\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{r}(\beta+k)}=r\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{rl}{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}l\beta}(-1)^{l(r-1)}

and the right-hand side equals

r\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}m\beta}F_{rm}{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}m}.

So if
r
is even then (
3.18
) is verified directly and if
r
is odd, then the equality is true because
F_{rl}=0
for odd
l
.

3.6
Surgeries on knots

Consider
M=S^{3}_{p}(K)
and assume that
-p\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
for concreteness. Then recalling (see Section
2.2
) that a spin
c
structure on
S^{3}_{p}(K)
can be encoded by an integer congruent to
p\mod 2
and that two integers give the same structure iff they differ by a multiple of
2p
, the general surgery formula (
2.33
) reads:

\widehat{Z}_{a}\big[S^{3}_{p}(K)\big]=q^{-\frac{3+p}{4}}\sum_{\ell=a\bmod 2p}F_{\ell}q^{-\frac{\ell^{2}}{4p}},

(3.19)

where the coefficients
F_{\ell}
appear in the expansion of

F(x,q):=F_{K}\big(x^{2},q\big)\big(x-x^{-1}\big)=\sum_{\ell}F_{\ell}x^{\ell}

(3.20)

with
F_{K}
introduced in
[
40
]
. We will use the facts that
F_{K}\big(x^{-2},q\big)=-F_{K}\big(x^{2},q\big)
and that
F_{K}\big(x^{2},q\big)\in x\mathbb{Z}\big[\big[x^{\pm 2},q\big]\big]
. In particular, it follows that
\widehat{Z}_{a}\equiv 0
for
a=1\bmod 2
.
As argued in
[
39
]
, the series
F_{K}(x,q)
gives ADO polynomials at roots of unity (which, in turn, are related to the CGP invariants for knot complements). Note, this already establishes a relation between
F_{K}(x,q):=\widehat{Z}\big(S^{3}\setminus K\big)
and
\mathrm{N}_{r}(K)
for knots, and tells us the relation between the parameters: in order to obtain
\mathrm{N}_{r}
on the CGP side this large class of examples shows that on the GPPV side we need to take
q={\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}/r}
(not
q={\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}/r}
).

With our choice of normalization, Conjecture
1.1

(b)
states that

\left.F_{K}(x,q)\right|_{q=\xi^{2}}=\frac{\operatorname{ADO}_{r}(K)\big(x/\xi^{2}\big)}{\Delta_{K}(x^{r})}\cdot\big(x^{1/2}-x^{-1/2}\big),\qquad\xi:={\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}/r},

(3.21)

where
\Delta_{K}(t)
denotes the Alexander polynomial of
K
.
We wish to compose this with the relation between ADO polynomials and CGP invariants for knot complements

\operatorname{ADO}_{r}(K)\big(x^{2}/\xi^{2}\big)=\frac{x^{r}-x^{-r}}{x-x^{-1}}\mathrm{N}_{r}(K_{\alpha}),\qquad\text{where}\quad x={\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\alpha}{r}}.

Eliminating the ADO polynomial from the above two relations we get a more direct relation between GPPV and CGP invariants for knot complements

\mathrm{N}_{r}(K_{\alpha})=\frac{F_{K}\big({\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}\alpha}{r}},{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}\big)\Delta_{K}\big({\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\alpha}\big)}{\big({\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\alpha}-{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}\alpha}\big)}.

(3.22)

This is the particular case of the more general Conjecture
2.13

(b)
combined with Conjecture
2.14
, in the case of the knot with zero framing.
On the other hand, we have

\mathrm{N}_{r}\big(S^{3}_{p}(K),\omega\big)=\frac{1}{\Delta_{-}}\sum_{k\in H_{r}}d(\alpha_{k})\mathrm{N}_{r}(K_{\alpha_{k}})T(\alpha_{k})^{p},

(3.23)

where
\mathrm{N}_{r}(K_{\alpha})
denotes CGP invariant of a (zero framing) knot in
S^{3}
colored by
\alpha
(e.g., for unknot
U
with zero framing
\mathrm{N}_{r}(U_{\alpha})=d(\alpha)
). The other notations are the same as in Section
2.4
. In particular,
\alpha_{k}:=\alpha+\mu
, where
\mu=\omega(\mathfrak{m})\in\frac{2}{p}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathfrak{m}\in H_{1}\big(S^{3}_{p}(K);\mathbb{Z}\big)\cong\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
is the generator represented by the meridian of the knot
K
.

We want to check that the surgery formulas for homological blocks
\widehat{Z}
and CGP invariant
\mathrm{N}_{r}
are consistent with the conjectural relations between these invariants for knot complements and closed manifolds. In other words, we want to check commutativity of the following schematic diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{Z}_{b}\big[S^{3}_{p}(K)\big](q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{N}_{r}\big(S^{3}_{p}(K),\omega\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{K}(x,q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{N}_{r}(K_{\alpha}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Laplace}\\
\text{transform}\end{subarray}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Kirby}\\
\text{color}\end{subarray}}

where, more concretely, the left vertical arrow is given by the equations (
3.19
)–(
3.20
), the right vertical arrow is given by (
3.23
), the top horizontal arrow is given by (
3.12
), (
3.10
) and (
3.13
), and the bottom horizontal arrow is given by (
3.22
).

This check can be done by essentially repeating the analysis done for plumbings in case of a single vertex (with
F
in (
3.3
) replaced by (
3.20
)) and using the following well known relation between the Reidemeister torsion of
M=S^{3}_{p}(K)
and the Alexander polynomial of the knot
K
:

\mathcal{T}\big(S^{3}_{p}(K),t\big)=\frac{t\Delta_{K}(t)}{(1-t)^{2}}\bigg|_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subset\operatorname{U}(1)},

(3.24)

where
t
is the holonomy of
\operatorname{U}(1)
flat connection along the the meridian
\mathfrak{m}
of the knot
K
. But let us write it explicitly anyway.

As for plumbings, after plugging (
2.29
) into (
3.23
), it is instructive to separate the result into three factors:

\mathrm{N}_{r}\big(S^{3}_{p}(K),\omega\big)=\mathcal{A}\cdot\mathcal{B}\cdot\mathcal{C},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=r^{-1/2}\xi^{-\frac{3+p}{2}}\cdot\begin{cases}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}p}{2}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}},&r=1\bmod 4,\\
2^{-1/2}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}},&r=2\bmod 4,\\
-{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}p}{2}},&r=3\bmod 4,\end{cases}

(3.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}=\frac{\Delta_{K}\big({\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\mu}\big)}{\big({\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu}-{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}\mu}\big)^{2}},

(3.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}=\sum_{k\in H_{r}}F\big(\xi^{\mu+k},\xi^{2}\big)\cdot\xi^{\frac{p}{2}(\mu+k)^{2}},

(3.27)

where, as before,
\xi:={\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{r}}
.
Consider a contribution of a monomial
x^{\ell}
from
F_{K}(x)
into (
3.27
)

\mathcal{C}_{\ell}:=\sum_{k\in H_{r}}\xi^{\ell(\mu+k)}\cdot\xi^{\frac{p}{2}(\mu+k)^{2}}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}p}{2r}(\tilde{\mu}+2n)^{2}+\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{r}\ell(\tilde{\mu}+2n)},

(3.28)

where

\tilde{\mu}:=\mu+(r-1).

We can now use the following one-dimensional Gauss reciprocity formula:

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\exp\bigg(\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}\big(pn^{2}+\ell n\big)\bigg)={\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\operatorname{sign}(p)}{4}}\sqrt{\frac{r}{2|p|}}\sum_{\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}/2p\mathbb{Z}}\exp\biggl(-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2p}\bigg(\tilde{a}+\frac{\ell}{r}\bigg)^{2}\biggr).

Applying it to (
3.28
) we have

\mathcal{C}_{\ell}=\xi^{-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2p}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}}\sqrt{\frac{r}{2|p|}}\sum_{\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}/2p\mathbb{Z}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2p}\tilde{a}^{2}-\pi{\rm i}\tilde{a}(\ell/p+\tilde{\mu})}.

Therefore, taking into account (
3.19
) we can write

\mathcal{C}=\sum_{\ell}\mathcal{C}_{\ell}F_{\ell}={\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}}\xi^{\frac{3+p}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{r}{2|p|}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\\
\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}/2p\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2p}\tilde{a}^{2}-\frac{2\pi{\rm i}\tilde{a}b}{p}-\pi{\rm i}\tilde{\mu}\tilde{a}}\widehat{Z}_{2b}\big[S^{3}_{p}(K)\big]\Big|_{q\rightarrow\xi^{2}}.

Combining this together with (
3.25
) and (
3.26
), we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}\big(S^{3}_{p}(K),\omega\big)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{{|p|}}}\frac{\Delta_{K}({\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\mu})}{({\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu}-{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}\mu})^{2}}\left\{\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}2^{-1},&r=2\bmod 4\\
2^{-1/2}{\rm e}^{\mp\frac{\pi{\rm i}p}{2}}{\rm e}^{\mp\frac{3\pi{\rm i}}{4}},&r=\pm 1\bmod 4\end{array}\!\!\right\}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\\
\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}/2p\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2p}\tilde{a}^{2}-\pi{\rm i}(r-1)\tilde{a}-\frac{2\pi{\rm i}\tilde{a}b}{p}-\pi{\rm i}{\mu}\tilde{a}}\widehat{Z}_{2b}\big[S^{3}_{p}(K)\big]\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}.

Using the formula (
3.24
) with
t={\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\mu}
, we indeed arrive at the conjectural formula (
3.10
), (
3.12
) or (
3.13
), depending on the value
r\bmod 4
, in the case of
M=S^{3}_{p}(K)
. For example, when
r=2\bmod 4
the sum over
\tilde{a}
reduces to the sum over
a=\tilde{a}\bmod p
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}\big(S^{3}_{p}(K),\omega\big)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{|p|}}}\frac{\Delta_{K}\big({\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\mu}\big)}{\big({\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu}-{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}\mu}\big)^{2}}\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\\
a\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\!\!{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2p}{a}^{2}-\pi{\rm i}a-\frac{2\pi{\rm i}ab}{p}-\pi{\rm i}{\mu}a}\widehat{Z}_{2b}\big[S^{3}_{p}(K)\big]\Big|_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}.\!\!\!

(3.29)

Similarly, for
r=1\bmod 4
,

c^{\mathrm{CGP}}_{a,b}=\frac{1}{|p|}\frac{\Delta_{K}\big({\rm e}^{4\pi{\rm i}a/p}\big)}{({\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}a/p}-{\rm e}^{-2\pi{\rm i}a/p})^{2}}{\rm e}^{-2\pi{\rm i}\frac{3\operatorname{sign}(p)-p}{4}}\sum_{c,f=0}^{p-1}{\rm e}^{-\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{p}(-f^{2}+ac+(b-f)c+(r-1)\frac{c^{2}}{4})}

and for
r=3
mod 4,

c^{\mathrm{CGP}}_{a,b}=\frac{1}{|p|}\frac{\Delta_{K}\big({\rm e}^{4\pi{\rm i}a/p}\big)}{\big({\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}a/p}-{\rm e}^{-2\pi{\rm i}a/p}\big)^{2}}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\frac{3\operatorname{sign}(p)-p}{4}}\sum_{c,f=0}^{p-1}{\rm e}^{-\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{p}(f^{2}+ac+(b+f)c+(r+1)\frac{c^{2}}{4})},

where we use the notation of (
1.6
).

Example 3.2
.

As a concrete example, consider
p=-3
surgery on the right-handed trefoil
K={\bf 3_{1}^{r}}
. In this case,
H_{1}(S^{3}_{p}(K))=\mathbb{Z}_{3}
and, therefore, there are two independent
\widehat{Z}
-invariants, which can be expressed in terms of the false theta-functions (cf.
[
19
,
40
]
):

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{0}=q^{\frac{71}{72}}\big(\tilde{\Psi}_{18}^{(1)}+\tilde{\Psi}_{18}^{(17)}\big)=q+q^{5}-q^{6}-q^{18}+q^{20}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{\pm 1}=-\frac{1}{2}q^{\frac{71}{72}}\big(\tilde{\Psi}_{18}^{(5)}+\tilde{\Psi}_{18}^{(13)}\big)=-\frac{1}{2}q^{4/3}\big(1+q^{2}-q^{7}-q^{13}+q^{23}+\cdots\big),

where the factor
\frac{1}{2}
in the latter expression and “
\pm
” in its label appear precisely because we write these expressions in the
unfolded
form.
Evaluating the right-hand side of (
3.29
) for various values of
r
, we find

\displaystyle r=5\colon

\displaystyle\phantom{-2}4.85591-6.4514\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=13\colon

\displaystyle-37.2754-1.28057\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=6\colon

\displaystyle\phantom{-2}5.30731-4.45336\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=14\colon

\displaystyle-11.4885+28.4093\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=7\colon

\displaystyle\phantom{-2}1.89035+3.49675\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=15\colon

\displaystyle-15.3891+13.8158\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=9\colon

\displaystyle-6.77162-0.394402\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=17\colon

\displaystyle-11.2632+37.6555\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=10\colon\quad

\displaystyle-24.2779+7.76375\mathrm{i},\qquad\quad

\displaystyle r=18\colon\quad

\displaystyle\phantom{-2}17.4965+18.5452\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=11\colon

\displaystyle-6.01733+3.60533\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle r=19\colon

\displaystyle\phantom{-2}59.3259+18.3538\mathrm{i},

\displaystyle\cdots\cdots

which match the corresponding values of
\mathrm{N}_{r}(S^{3}_{-3}({\bf 3_{1}^{r}}))
.

3.7
0-surgeries on knots

In general, the invariant of a knot complement is usually written as

F_{K}(x,q)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\geq 1\\
\text{odd}\end{subarray}}f_{m}(q)\cdot\big(x^{\frac{m}{2}}-x^{-\frac{m}{2}}\big),

(3.30)

which after multiplying by
\big(x^{\frac{1}{2}}-x^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)
in the surgery formula gives

\big(x^{\frac{1}{2}}-x^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)F_{K}(x,q)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\geq 1\\
\text{odd}\end{subarray}}f_{m}(q)\cdot\big(x^{\frac{m}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}-x^{\frac{m}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}-x^{-\frac{m}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}+x^{-\frac{m}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}\big),

which means (with
n\in\mathbb{Z}
):

\text{Coeff}_{x^{n}}\Big[\big(x^{\frac{1}{2}}-x^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)F_{K}(x,q)\Big]=\begin{cases}f_{2n-1}-f_{2n+1}&\text{if}\quad n\geq 1,\\
-2f_{1}&\text{if}\quad n=0,\\
f_{2|n|-1}-f_{2|n|+1}&\text{if}\quad n\leq-1.\end{cases}

(3.31)

For the unknot we have
f_{m}(q)=\delta_{m,1}
, so that
\widehat{Z}_{n}=\{\ldots,0,0,1,-2,1,0,0,\ldots\}
, where by
\widehat{Z}_{n}
we denote the invariant
\widehat{Z}_{\mathfrak{s}}\big(S^{3}(K)\big)
associated to the spin
c
structure encoded by the integer
2n
on the knot (see Section
2.2
).

Example 3.3
.

The right-handed trefoil knot
K={\bf 3_{1}^{r}}
: consider the
0
-framed trefoil and let
H_{r}=\{-(r-1),-(r-3),\ldots,(r-1)\}
we have

\mathrm{N}_{r}\big(({\bf 3_{1}^{r}})_{\alpha}\big)=\frac{(-1)^{r-1}\xi^{9(r-1)^{2}/4}}{\{2r\alpha\}}\sum_{n\in H_{r}}\xi^{3n\alpha+\frac{3n^{2}}{4}}\{2\alpha+n\}

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{r}(S^{3}_{0}({\bf 3_{1}^{r}}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in H_{r}}d(\alpha+k)\frac{(-1)^{r-1}\xi^{9(r-1)^{2}/4}}{\{2r\alpha+2rk\}}\sum_{n\in H_{r}}\xi^{3n\alpha+3nk+\frac{3n^{2}}{4}}\{2\alpha+2k+n\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{r-1}\frac{\xi^{9(r-1)^{2}/4}}{\{r\alpha\}\{2r\alpha\}}\sum_{k\in H_{r}}\sum_{n\in H_{r}}(-1)^{k}\{\alpha+k\}\{2\alpha+2k+n\}\xi^{3n\alpha+3nk+\frac{3n^{2}}{4}}.

On the other hand, for the right-handed trefoil knot
K={\bf 3_{1}^{r}}
we have
f_{m}=\epsilon_{m}q^{\frac{m^{2}+23}{24}}
, where

\epsilon_{m}=\begin{cases}-1&\text{if}\quad m\equiv 1\text{ or }11\!\!\!\pmod{12},\\
+1&\text{if}\quad m\equiv 5\text{ or }7\!\!\!\pmod{12},\\
\phantom{-}0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

and so

\widehat{Z}_{n}=\begin{cases}\epsilon_{2n-1}q^{\frac{(2n-1)^{2}+23}{24}}-\epsilon_{2n+1}q^{\frac{(2n+1)^{2}+23}{24}}&\text{if}\quad n\geq 1,\\[2.84526pt]
-2\epsilon_{1}q&\text{if}\quad n=0,\\[2.84526pt]
\epsilon_{2|n|-1}q^{\frac{(2|n|-1)^{2}+23}{24}}-\epsilon_{2|n|+1}q^{\frac{(2|n|+1)^{2}+23}{24}}&\text{if}\quad n\leq-1.\end{cases}

Again, we verified numerically and analytically that the proposed relations hold.

3.8
Surgery on a link

Let
L
be the link
L_{9}27
in the Thistlethwaite table of links (see also Knot Atlas).
Let
M
be obtained by
a,b,c\in\mathbb{N}
integral surgery on the three components of
L
, where
a
corresponds to the blue component,
b
to the purple and
c
to the green one in Figure
2
.
The linking matrix of
L
is diagonal with entries
a
,
b
,
c
so that a cohomology class
\omega\in H^{1}(M;\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})
is described by a three-uple
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
with
\alpha\in\big\{\frac{2k}{a},k=0,\ldots,a-1\big\}
,
\beta\in\{\frac{2k}{b},\,k=0,\ldots,b-1\}
and
\gamma\in\big\{\frac{2k}{c},\,k=0,\ldots,c-1\big\}
.

Figure 2:
The
L_{9}27
link (Knotscape image).

L
is one of the first links with the peculiar property that its multicolored Alexander polynomial is zero.
Thus we have
\mathrm{N}_{2}(M,\omega)=0
for any
\omega\in H^{1}(M;\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})
.

On the other hand, a direct computer-based calculation gives

\mathrm{ADO}_{3}(L)(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\xi^{2\gamma}+\big(1-{\rm i}\sqrt{3}\big)+\xi^{-2\gamma}

so that in particular it does not depend on
\alpha
,
\beta
and we will denote it
P(\gamma)
.
As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{N}_{3}(M,\omega)={}

\displaystyle\Delta_{+}^{-3}\bigg(\sum_{k_{1}\in\{-2,0,2\}}\xi^{\frac{a}{2}((\alpha+k_{1})^{2}-(r-1)^{2})}d(\alpha+k_{1})\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{k_{2}\in\{-2,0,2\}}\xi^{\frac{b}{2}((\beta+k_{2})^{2}-(r-1)^{2})}d(\beta+k_{2})\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\sum_{k_{3}\in\{-2,0,2\}}\xi^{\frac{c-1}{2}((\gamma+k_{3})^{2}-(r-1)^{2})}d(\gamma+k_{3})P(\gamma+k_{3})\bigg)

(in the last factor of the above expression we used
c-1
as the self linking of the green component is
1
in the given diagram).
Choosing for instance
a=b=c=4
and
\alpha=\beta=\gamma=\frac{1}{2}
one can compute directly that
\mathrm{N}_{3}(M,\omega)\neq 0
. Since the torsion is
0
as the multivariable Alexander polynomial is, then in this case the invariants
\widehat{Z}
should have infinite limits when
q\to\exp(2{\rm i}\frac{\pi}{3})
.

4
The relation between
\bm{\mathrm{N}_{r}}
and
\bm{\widehat{Z}}

In this section, we present a more general and systematic discussion of the proposed relation.

4.1
Gauss sum vs Laplace transform

Let
M
be the rational homology sphere obtained by a surgery on a framed link
L
in
S^{3}
with a linking matrix
B
. We will use the shorthand notations
x\equiv\{x_{I}\}_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}
,
x^{\pm}\equiv\big\{x_{I}^{\pm 1}\big\}_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}
. In particular,
K[[x^{\pm}]]
denotes the space of formal Laurent series in
x_{I}^{\pm}
with coefficients in
K
, considered as a module over the ring of Laurent polynomials
K[x^{\pm}]
.

As before,
\omega\in H^{1}(M;\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})
and
\mu_{I}=\omega(\mathfrak{m}_{I})
, which we assume to be fixed. We also use notation

\xi\equiv{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{r}}

in what follows.

Let
K
be a field extension over
\mathbb{C}
(some of the relevant cases are
K=\mathbb{C}
,
K=\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)
,
K=\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)(x)
). By
K(t)^{\prime}
we will denote the localization of
K[t]
by the subset of polynomials in
t
non-vanishing at
t=1
. This is a subset of the field
K(t)
of rational functions in
t
closed under multiplication. One can then consider the following ring homomorphism:

Definition 4.1
.

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\colon\ K(t)^{\prime}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K,

\displaystyle\frac{P(t)}{Q(t)}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\frac{P(1)}{Q(1)}.

Similarly, by
K(x)^{\prime}
we will denote the localization of
K[x^{\pm}]
by the subset of Laurent polynomials non-vanishing at
x=\xi^{\mu+k}
for any
k\in H_{r}^{\mathrm{Vert}}
. This is a subset of the field
K(x)
of rational functions in
x
closed under multiplication. We can then define the following
K
-linear “
\omega
-twisted Gauss sum” operation:

Definition 4.2

(
\omega
-twisted Gauss sum)
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}\colon\ K(x)^{\prime}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K,

\displaystyle\frac{P(x)}{Q(x)}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum\limits_{k\in H_{r}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\frac{P(\xi^{\mu+k})}{Q(\xi^{\mu+k})}\xi^{\frac{1}{2}(\mu+k)^{T}B(\mu+k)}.

Denote
p:=4|\det B|
and let
\mathbb{C}((q^{1/p}))\equiv\mathbb{C}\big[\big[q^{1/p},q^{-1/p}\big]
be the field of fractions of
\mathbb{C}\big[\big[q^{1/p}\big]\big]
.
Then define the
\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)
-linear “
\omega
-twisted Laplace transform” on a subspace of
\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)[[x^{\pm}]]
by the following formula:

Definition 4.3

(
\omega
-twisted Laplace transform)
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}\colon\ \ \mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)\big[\big[x^{\pm}\big]\big]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big),

(4.1)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}A_{\ell,m}q^{\frac{m}{p}}x^{\ell}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}q^{\frac{n}{p}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}\\
4m-p\ell^{T}B^{-1}\ell=4n\end{subarray}}A_{\ell,m}\cdot\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\omega},

where

\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\omega}=\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}(r/2)^{V/2}}{|\det B|^{1/2}}\sum_{\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/2B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}\tilde{a}^{T}B^{-1}\tilde{a}-\pi{\rm i}\tilde{a}^{T}B^{-1}(\ell+B(\mu+(r-1)\varepsilon))}.

We say that
\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}
is well defined if the interior sum in the right-hand side of (
4.1
) has a finite number of non-zero terms.

Remark 4.4
.

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}
restricted on
\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)\big[x^{\pm}\big]
is well-defined.

Remark 4.5
.

Apart from
\omega\in H^{1}(M;\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})
(equivalently,
\mu\in(\mathbb{C}/2\mathbb{Z})^{\mathrm{Vert}}
s.t.
B\mu=0\bmod 2\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}
), the operations
\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}
also (implicitly) depend on
B
and
r
.

Next we introduce the following limit operation (a morphism of
\mathbb{C}
-algebras).

Definition 4.6
.

\displaystyle\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\colon\ \mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{C},

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{m}A_{m}q^{\frac{m}{p}}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\ \lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\sum\limits_{m}A_{m}q^{\frac{m}{p}}.

We say that the operation is well defined if the power series in
q^{1/p}
are convergent for
0<|q^{1/p}|<1
and the limit, taken to the root of unity along the radial direction, exists and is finite.

Remark 4.7
.

This operation can be extended to the polynomials/series with coefficients in
\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)
by applying it coefficient-wise.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
A(x,q)\in\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)\big[x^{\pm}\big]
such that
\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}A(x,q)\in\mathbb{C}\big[x^{\pm}\big]
exists. Then

\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}A(x,q)=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}A(x,q)=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}A(x,q).

Proof.

The first equality follows from the definition of
\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}
, which involves taking a finite linear combination of the evaluations at certain values of
x
. The second equality
is shown by applying the Gauss reciprocity formula (
3.5
) to individual monomials in
x
, the number of which is finite.∎

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
A(x)\in K(x)^{\prime}
,
B(x^{r})\in K(x)^{\prime}\cap K(x^{r})
. Then

\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}(B(x^{r})A(x))=B((-1)^{r-1}{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu})\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}(A(x)).

Proof.

Follows from the definition of
\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}
.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

The statement of the Proposition
4.9
is directly extended to the series in
K^{\prime}(x)((t))
by applying it coefficient-wise.

We also define the following limiting operation on a subalgebra of
\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)((t))
:

Definition 4.11
.

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\colon\qquad\qquad\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)((t))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big),

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n}\bigg(\sum\limits_{m}A_{n,m}q^{\frac{m}{p}}\bigg)t^{n}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum\limits_{m}\bigg(\sum\limits_{n}A_{n,m}\bigg)q^{\frac{m}{p}}.

We say that the operation is well defined if there is a finite number of non-vanishing coefficients
A_{n,m}
for any fixed
m
.

Remark 4.12
.

When restricted on the subspace
C\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)(t)^{\prime}\subset C\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)((t))
and is well-defined, the result of the operation in Definition
4.11
coincides with the result of the operation in Definition
4.1
.

With such definitions, consider the following diagram of (partially defined) algebra homomorphisms, where the dotted arrow means that we make the hypothesis that in the cases of interest the image of
\lim_{q\to{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}/r}}
map is contained in the subspace
\mathbb{C}(t)^{\prime}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)((t))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}((t))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}(t)^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}}

(4.2)

Proposition 4.13
.

When
r\neq 0\bmod 4
and

\ell=2b+B(s-\varepsilon)\bmod 2B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}},

where
b
and
s
represent elements of
H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})
and
\operatorname{Spin}(M)
respectively, the coefficients
\mathcal{C}^{\omega}_{\ell}
in the Definition
4.3
admit the following expression:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\omega}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}r^{V/2}}{|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}

\displaystyle\times\begin{cases}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}(5\sigma-2\operatorname{Tr}B)}\sum\limits_{a,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r-1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)+{\ell k}(a,f-b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)+{\ell k}(f,f)-\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 284.52756ptr=1\bmod 4,\\
2^{V/2}|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|^{1/2}\sum\limits_{a\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(a)-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(a,b)-\pi{\rm i}\omega(a)},\qquad\ r=2\bmod 4,\\[8.53581pt]
{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}(-5\sigma+2\operatorname{Tr}B)}\sum\limits_{a,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r+1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)-{\ell k}(a,f+b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)-{\ell k}(f,f)+\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 284.52756ptr=3\bmod 4.\end{cases}

Proof.

Is contained in Section
3.1
.
∎

Assuming the identification
\xi^{\alpha_{I}}=x_{I}
, one can consider (see (
2.24
) and (
2.23
)):

\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{T}B\alpha}\mathrm{N}_{r}(L_{\alpha})\in\mathbb{C}(x)^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{C}(x).

Proposition 4.14
.

\frac{1}{\Delta_{+}^{b_{+}}\Delta_{-}^{b_{-}}}\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}\bigg(\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{T}B\alpha}\mathrm{N}_{r}(L_{\alpha})\cdot\frac{\prod_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}\big(x_{I}-x^{-1}_{I}\big)}{\prod_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}\big(x_{I}^{r}-x^{-r}_{I}\big)}\bigg)=\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega).

(4.3)

Proof.

After using the identification
x_{I}=\xi^{\alpha_{I}}
, taking into accound the formula (
2.19
) for the modified quantum dimensions, and writing explicitly the action of
\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}
according to the formula in the Definition
4.2
, the left-hand side of (
4.3
) becomes the surgery formula (
2.25
) for
\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)
.
∎

Proposition 4.15
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}\bigg(F_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\cdot\prod_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}\big(x_{I}-x^{-1}_{I}\big)\bigg)={}\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}r^{V/2}}{|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}\begin{cases}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}(5\sigma-2\operatorname{Tr}B)}\!\!\sum\limits_{a,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}\!\!\!{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r-1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)+{\ell k}(a,f-b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)+{\ell k}(f,f)-\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 284.52756ptr=1\bmod 4,\\[2.84526pt]
2^{V/2}|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|^{1/2}\sum\limits_{a\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(a)-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(a,b)-\pi{\rm i}\omega(a)},\qquad\ r=2\bmod 4,\\[5.69054pt]
{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}(-5\sigma+2\operatorname{Tr}B)}\!\!\sum\limits_{a,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}\!\!\!{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r+1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)-{\ell k}(a,f+b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)-{\ell k}(f,f)+\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 284.52756ptr=3\bmod 4,\\
\end{cases}

\displaystyle\times(-1)^{b_{+}}q^{\frac{\operatorname{Tr}B-3\sigma}{4}}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b,s)}

when the left-hand side is well defined.

Proof.

Using the expansion (
2.32
), the left-hand side reads:

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}\bigg(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}F_{\ell}x^{\ell}\bigg)=\sum_{\ell}C_{\ell}^{\omega}F_{\ell}q^{-\frac{\ell^{T}B^{-1}\ell}{4}}=\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=2b+B(s-\varepsilon)\\
\bmod 2B\mathbb{Z}^{V}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\omega}F_{\ell}q^{-\frac{\ell^{t}B^{-1}\ell}{4}},

(4.4)

where in the first equality we used Definition
4.3
. In the second equality, we split the sum over
\ell
into a sum over
\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}/B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}\cong H_{1}(M,\mathbb{Z})
and the sum over
\ell
with fixed values modulo
2B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}
. Proposition
4.13
provides an explicit formula for the coefficients
\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\omega}
, which depend only on the value
\ell
modulo
2B\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}
. Combined with the surgery formula (
2.34
) for
\widehat{Z}
in the case of a rational homology sphere it gives us the formula in the statement of the proposition.
∎

Note that
t
-regularization can be understood as the following
\mathbb{C}
-linear map:

Definition 4.16
.

\displaystyle(\,\cdot\,)^{t}\colon\qquad\qquad\quad K[[x^{\pm}]]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K[x^{\pm}][[t]],

\displaystyle f(x)=\sum\limits_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}}f_{\ell}x^{\ell}

\displaystyle\longmapsto f^{t}(x)=\sum\limits_{m\geq 0}\bigg(\sum\limits_{\ell\colon\|\ell\|=m}f_{\ell}x^{\ell}\bigg)t^{m},

where
\|\cdot\|
is the
L^{1}
norm.

The target space of this operation is an integral domain, and its ring of fractions is a subfield of
K(x)((t))
. In particular,

\frac{F^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)}{F^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2r},q^{r}\big)}\in\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)(x)((t)).

The Conjecture
2.13
then states that
\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{{2\pi{\rm i}}/{r}}}
takes it to an element in

\mathbb{C}(x)(t)^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{C}(x)(t)\subset\mathbb{C}(x)((t)),

and
\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}
then takes it further to

\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{T}B\alpha+\frac{(r-1)^{2}\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}\mathrm{N}_{r}(L_{\alpha})\in\mathbb{C}(x)^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{C}(x).

Theorem 4.17
.

Let
M=S^{3}(L)
and
W_{L}(x,q)
be such that Conjecture
2.13
, part
(a)
, holds with the following additional assumptions:

(i)

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)
is well-defined and
\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}
when applied to the ratio

\frac{\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{\big(\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}|_{r=1}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)\big)|_{q\rightarrow q^{r}}},

that is the maps in the diagram (
4.2
) commute when restricted to this element in the top left corner.

(ii)

\exists\alpha(q,t)\in\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)((t))
such that
\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}}}\alpha(q,t)W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\in\mathbb{C}[x^{\pm}]((t))
exists and is non-zero.

Then there exist
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)\in 2^{-c}q^{\Delta}\mathbb{Z}[[q]]
,
\mathfrak{s}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(M)
such that

\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\frac{\sum\limits_{b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}C^{r}_{\omega,b}W_{\sigma(b,s)}(q)}{\sum\limits_{b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\omega(b)}W_{\sigma(b,s)}(q^{r})},

where

\displaystyle C^{r}_{\omega,b}:=\frac{1}{|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{a,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r-1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)+{\ell k}(a,f-b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)+{\ell k}(f,f)-\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 247.53897ptr=1\bmod 4,\\
|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|^{1/2}\sum\limits_{a\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(a)-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(a,b)-\pi{\rm i}\omega(a)},\ \ \,r=2\bmod 4,\\[11.38109pt]
\sum\limits_{a,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r+1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)-{\ell k}(a,f+b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)-{\ell k}(f,f)+\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 247.53897ptr=3\bmod 4.\end{cases}

Proof.

The Conjecture
2.13
, part
(a)
, states that

\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{T}B\alpha+\frac{(r-1)^{2}\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}\mathrm{N}_{r}(L_{\alpha})=\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\frac{W_{L}^{t}\big(x^{2};q\big)}{W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2r};q^{r}\big)}.

Multiplying both sides by
\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)/\big(x_{I}^{r}-x_{I}^{-r}\big)
and applying
\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}
we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}\bigg(\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{T}B\alpha+\frac{(r-1)^{2}\operatorname{Tr}B}{2}}\mathrm{N}_{r}(L_{\alpha})\prod_{I}\frac{\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{\big(x_{I}^{r}-x_{I}^{-r}\big)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\frac{W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2r},q^{r}\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}^{r}-x_{I}^{-r}\big)},

(4.5)

where we could bring
\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}
inside the limit since its definition involves taking a finite sum of evaluations of the rational functions in
x
that appear in the coefficients of the series. By Proposition
4.14
, the left-hand side of the equation (
4.5
) gives the left-hand side of the equation in the statement of the theorem, up to a simple factor.
In the right-hand side, inside the limit
\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}
we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}

\displaystyle\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\frac{W_{L}^{t}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{W_{L}^{t}\big(x^{2r};q^{r}\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}^{r}-x_{I}^{-r}\big)}=\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}\frac{\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}W_{L}^{t}\big(x^{2},q\big)\alpha(q^{r},t)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}W_{L}^{t}\big(x^{2r},q^{r}\big)\alpha(q^{r},t)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}^{r}-x_{I}^{-r}\big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W_{L}^{t}\big(x^{2},q\big)\alpha(q^{r},t)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}}}W_{L}^{t}\big({\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\mu},q\big)\alpha(q,t)\prod_{I}\big({\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}-{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}\big)},

(4.6)

where in the first equality we used the the assumption (ii) of the theorem. In the second equality we used the results of the Propositions
4.9
and
4.8
.

Using Proposition
4.8
for
r=1
, we have

\displaystyle\xi^{\frac{1}{2}\mu^{T}B\mu}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}}}W_{L}^{t}\big({\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\mu},q\big)\alpha(q,t)\prod_{I}\big({\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}-{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}\mu_{I}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}}}\mathfrak{G}_{\omega}|_{r=1}W_{L}^{t}\big(x^{2},q\big)\alpha(q,t)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}}}\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}|_{r=1}W_{L}^{t}\big(x^{2},q\big)\alpha(q,t)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big).

It follows that the right-hand side of (
4.5
) is equal to

\displaystyle\xi^{\frac{1}{2}\mu^{T}B\mu}\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\frac{\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{\big(\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}|_{r=1}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)\big)\big|_{q\rightarrow q^{r}}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\xi^{\frac{1}{2}\mu^{T}B\mu}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\frac{\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{\big(\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}|_{r=1}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)\big)\big|_{q\rightarrow q^{r}}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\xi^{\frac{1}{2}\mu^{T}B\mu}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\frac{\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{\big(\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}|_{r=1}W_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)\big)\big|_{q\rightarrow q^{r}}},

(4.7)

where we used the assumption (i).

Let
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)
be defined through
W_{L}(x,q)
in the same way as
\widehat{Z}_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)
is defined through
F_{L}(x,q)
, i.e., surgery formula (
2.34
). From Proposition
4.15
with
r=1
, we then have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}|_{r=1}W_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{(-1)^{b_{+}}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}(3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B)}}{|H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}\sum\limits_{a,b,f\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left({\ell k}(a,f-b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)+{\ell k}(f,f)-\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)}W_{\sigma(b,s)}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=(-1)^{b_{+}}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}(3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B)}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}\mu^{T}B\mu}\sum\limits_{b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(\omega(b)-\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+{\ell k}(b,b)+\frac{1}{2}\right)}W_{\sigma(b,s)}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=(-1)^{b_{+}}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}(3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B)}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{2}\mu^{T}B\mu}\sum\limits_{b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\omega(b)}W_{\sigma(b,s)}\Big|_{q\rightarrow q{\rm e}^{-2\pi{\rm i}}},

(4.8)

where we have used the fact that, according to the formula (
2.34
), the overall rational power shift of
q
-series
W_{\sigma(b,s)}
modulo 1 is given by

\frac{3\sigma-\operatorname{Tr}B}{4}-\frac{(s-\varepsilon)^{T}B(s-\varepsilon)}{4}-b^{T}B^{-1}b=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mu(M,s)}{4}-{\ell k}(b,b)\bmod 1.

Using (
4.8
) in the right-hand side of (
4.7
) and also using the Proposition
4.15
for general
r
, we conclude that the right-hand side of (
4.5
) gives the the right-hand side of the equation in the statement of the theorem, up to a simple phase factor. Taking care of the phase factors on both sides of (
4.5
) concludes the proof of the first part of the statement of the theorem.

If Conjecture
2.13
, part
(b)
, holds, we take
W_{L}(x,q)=F_{L}(x,q)
, and then by construction
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)=\widehat{Z}_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)
.
∎

Theorem 4.18
.

Let
M=S^{3}(L)
and
W_{L}(x,q)
be such that Conjectures
2.13
and
2.14
, parts
(a)
, hold with the following additional assumption:

(i)

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)
is well defined and
\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}
when applied to

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)

that is the maps in the diagram (
4.2
) commute when restricted to this element in the top left corner.

Then there exist
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)\in 2^{-c}q^{\Delta}\mathbb{Z}[[q]]
,
\mathfrak{s}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(M)
such that

\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)=\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\sum_{b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}C^{r}_{\omega,b}W_{\sigma(b,s)},

where
C^{r}_{\omega,b}
are the same as in Theorem
4.17
.
Moreover, if the parts
(b)
of Conjectures
2.13
and
2.14
also hold and
F_{L}(x,q)
satisfies the assumption
(i)
, then one can take
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)=\widehat{Z}_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)
.

Proof.

One can follow the proof of the Theorem
4.17
since, from the Conjecture
2.14
, the assumption
(iv)
of that theorem automatically holds with
\alpha(q,t)=1
. Moreover, the Conjecture
2.14
provides a relation between
\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\lim_{q\rightarrow 1}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)
which appears in the denominator of (
4.6
) and the Alexander–Conway function.
Using the surgery formula for the torsion (see Appendix
A
), we arrive at the statement of the theorem.
∎

Remark 4.19
.

In the case of plumbing surgeries, the assumption (ii) holds if and only if the plumbing is
weakly negative definite
, meaning the inverse of the linking matrix,
B^{-1}
, restricted on the vertices of degree
>2
is negative definite (cf.
[
40
]
).

Example 4.20
.

Let
M
be a rational homology sphere obtained by surgery over a plumbing link corresponding to a “
Y
-shaped” plumbing graph i.e. one formed by a single trivalent vertex corresponding to an unknot with strictly negative framing, three
1
-valent vertices and some
2
-valent vertices.
Then as shown in Appendix
D
the hypothesis of Theorem
4.18
is satisfied.
So this provides an infinite family of examples in which Theorem
4.18
holds. This family of examples overlaps with the ones considered in
[
3
,
36
,
40
,
41
]
.

4.2
Generalization to
\bm{b_{1}\geq 0}

In this section, we briefly list modifications one needs to do in Section
4.1
in order to generalize the results to the case of general
b_{1}\geq 0
.
We will follow the conventions of Section
2.6
. In particular, we fix
U\in{\rm SL}(V,\mathbb{Z})
such that

UBU^{T}=\begin{pmatrix}B^{\prime}&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\!,

where
\det B^{\prime}\neq 0
and use the following notations:

\begin{pmatrix}\ell^{\prime}\\
\ell^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}:=U\ell,\qquad\begin{pmatrix}\mu^{\prime}\\
\mu^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}:=\big(U^{T}\big)^{-1}\mu,\qquad\begin{pmatrix}s^{\prime}\\
s^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}:=\big(U^{T}\big)^{-1}s,\qquad\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon^{\prime}\\
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}:=\big(U^{T}\big)^{-1}\varepsilon.

We redefine
p:=4|\det B^{\prime}|
.
Definitions
4.1
,
4.2
,
4.6
,

4.11
,
4.16
and Propositions
4.9
,
4.14
do not need to be modified. Definition
4.3
is generalized to

Definition 4.21
.

Define the “
\omega
-twisted Laplace transform” on a subspace of
\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)[[x^{\pm}]]
by the following formula:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}\colon\ \mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)\big[\big[x^{\pm}\big]\big]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big),

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}A_{\ell,m}q^{\frac{m}{p}}x^{\ell}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}q^{\frac{n}{p}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{Vert}}\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}\\
4m-p(\ell^{\prime})^{T}(B^{\prime})^{-1}\ell^{\prime}+2p\sum_{I}|\ell^{\prime\prime}_{I}||\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{I}|=4n\end{subarray}}A_{\ell,m}\cdot\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\omega},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\omega}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}(r/2)^{\frac{V-b_{1}}{2}}r^{b_{1}}}{|\det B^{\prime}|^{1/2}}{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}(\ell^{\prime\prime})^{T}(\epsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{\mu^{\prime\prime}}{r})}\delta_{\ell^{\prime\prime}=0\bmod r}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\tilde{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{V-b_{1}}/2B^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}^{V-b_{1}}}{\rm e}^{\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r}{2}\tilde{a}^{T}(B^{\prime})^{-1}\tilde{a}-\tilde{a}^{T}(B^{\prime})^{-1}(\ell^{\prime}+B^{\prime}(\mu^{\prime}+(r-1)\varepsilon^{\prime}))\right)}.

We say that
\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}
is well defined if the interior sum in the right-hand side of (
4.1
) has finite number of non-zero terms.

With such modified definitions Proposition
4.8
, then still holds by the similar argument. Propositions
4.13
and
4.15
are modified respectively to the following two:

Proposition 4.22
.

When
r\neq 0\bmod 4
and

\displaystyle\ell^{\prime}=2b^{\prime}+B^{\prime}(s^{\prime}-\varepsilon^{\prime})\bmod 2B^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}^{V-b_{1}},

\displaystyle\ell^{\prime\prime}=\mathrm{LCM}(r,2)m\in\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}},

where
b^{\prime}
and
s
represent elements of
\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})
and
\mathrm{Spin}(M)
, respectively, the coefficients
\mathcal{C}^{\omega}_{\ell}
in the Definition
4.21
admit the following expression:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\ell}^{\omega}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}r^{\frac{V+b_{1}}{2}}}{|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}\omega^{\prime\prime}(m)}{\mathrm{GCD}(r,2)}}

\displaystyle\times\begin{cases}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}(5\sigma-2\operatorname{Tr}B)}\sum\limits_{a,f\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r-1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)+{\ell k}(a,f-b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)+{\ell k}(f,f)-\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 310.13503ptr=1\bmod 4,\\
2^{\frac{V-b_{1}}{2}}|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|^{1/2}\sum\limits_{a\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(a)-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(a,b)-\pi{\rm i}\omega(a)},\ \ \ r=2\bmod 4,\\[8.53581pt]
{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}(-5\sigma+2\operatorname{Tr}B)}\sum\limits_{a,f\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r+1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)-{\ell k}(a,f+b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)-{\ell k}(f,f)+\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 310.13503ptr=3\bmod 4.\end{cases}\!\!\!\!

Proof.

It is contained in Section
3.1
.
∎

Proposition 4.23
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}

\displaystyle\bigg(F_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\cdot\prod_{I\in\mathrm{Vert}}\big(x_{I}-x^{-1}_{I}\big)\bigg)=\frac{{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}\sigma}{4}}r^{\frac{V+b_{1}}{2}}}{|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}}\end{subarray}}{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}\omega^{\prime\prime}(m)}{\mathrm{GCD}(r,2)}}

\displaystyle\times\begin{cases}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}(5\sigma-2\operatorname{Tr}B)}\sum\limits_{a,f\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r-1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)+{\ell k}(a,f-b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)+{\ell k}(f,f)-\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 312.9803ptr=1\bmod 4,\\
2^{\frac{V-b_{1}}{2}}|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|^{1/2}\sum\limits_{a\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(a)-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(a,b)-\pi{\rm i}\omega(a)},\quad r=2\bmod 4,\\[8.53581pt]
{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{4}(-5\sigma+2\operatorname{Tr}B)}\sum\limits_{a,f\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r+1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)-{\ell k}(a,f+b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)-{\ell k}(f,f)+\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 312.9803ptr=3\bmod 4\end{cases}

\displaystyle\times(-1)^{b_{+}}q^{\frac{\operatorname{Tr}B-3\sigma}{4}}\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b\oplus\frac{mr}{\mathrm{GCD}(r,2)},s)}

when the left-hand side is well defined.

Proof.

Follows from the definition of
\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}
, Proposition
4.22
and the conditional definition of
\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b,s)}
through
F_{L}
(equation (
2.33
)).
∎

Remark 4.24
.

In Proposition
4.23
and the theorems below,
\widehat{Z}_{\sigma(b^{\prime}\oplus b^{\prime\prime},s)}
is understood as a particular representative in
2^{-c}q^{\Delta}\mathbb{Z}[[q]]\subset\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)
, rather then an element of the quotient over the subspace
\big(1-q^{\mathrm{LCM}(2,\mathrm{GCD}(b^{\prime\prime}))}\big)\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)
. This representative is fixed by the choice of the surgery link
L
in the definition of
\widehat{Z}
via
F_{L}
by the formula (
2.33
).

Theorems
4.17
and
4.18
are then modified respectively to the following two, with proofs following similar arguments:

Theorem 4.25
.

Let
M=S^{3}(L)
and
W_{L}(x,q)
be such that Conjecture
2.13
, part
(a)
, holds with the following additional assumptions:

(i)

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)
is well-defined and
\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}
when applied to the ratio

\frac{\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)}{\left(\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}|_{r=1}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)\right)|_{q\rightarrow q^{r}}},

that is the maps in the diagram (
4.2
) commute when restricted to this element in the top left corner.

(ii)

\exists\alpha(q,t)\in\mathbb{C}\big(\big(q^{1/p}\big)\big)((t))
such that
\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}}}\alpha(q,t)W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\in\mathbb{C}[x^{\pm}]((t))
exists and is non-zero.

Then there exist
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)\in 2^{-c}q^{\Delta}\mathbb{Z}[[q]]
,
\mathfrak{s}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(M)
such that

\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\frac{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}}\end{subarray}}C^{r}_{\omega,b,m}W_{\sigma(b\oplus\frac{mr}{\mathrm{GCD}(r,2)},s)}}{\sum\limits_{b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\omega(b)}W_{\sigma(b,s)}(q^{r})},

where

\displaystyle C^{r}_{\omega,b,m}:={}

\displaystyle\frac{r^{b_{1}}{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}\omega^{\prime\prime}(m)}{\mathrm{GCD}(r,2)}}}{|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|}

\displaystyle\times\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{a,f\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r-1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)+{\ell k}(a,f-b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)+{\ell k}(f,f)-\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 284.52756ptr=1\bmod 4,\\
|\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})|^{1/2}\sum\limits_{a\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi{\rm i}r}{2}q_{s}(a)-2\pi{\rm i}{\ell k}(a,b)-\pi{\rm i}\omega(a)},\quad r=2\bmod 4,\\[5.69054pt]
\sum\limits_{a,f\in\operatorname{Tor}H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})}{\rm e}^{2\pi{\rm i}\left(-\frac{r+1}{4}{\ell k}(a,a)-{\ell k}(a,f+b)-\frac{1}{2}\omega(a)-{\ell k}(f,f)+\frac{1}{4}\mu(M,s)+\frac{1}{2}\right)},\\[-8.53581pt]
\hskip 284.52756ptr=3\bmod 4.\end{cases}\!\!\!\!

Moreover, if the parts
(b)
of Conjectures
2.13
and
2.14
also hold and
F_{L}(x,q)
satisfies the assumption
(i)
, then one can take
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)=\widehat{Z}_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)
.

Theorem 4.26
.

Let
M=S^{3}(L)
and
W_{L}(x,q)
be such that Conjectures
2.13
and
2.14
, parts
(a)
, hold with the following additional assumption:

(i)

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)
is well defined and
\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}=\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\lim_{t\rightarrow 1}
when applied to

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega}W^{t}_{L}\big(x^{2},q\big)\prod_{I}\big(x_{I}-x_{I}^{-1}\big)

that is the maps in the diagram (
4.2
) commute when restricted to this element in the top left corner.

Then there exist
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)\in 2^{-c}q^{\Delta}\mathbb{Z}[[q]]
,
\mathfrak{s}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(M)
such that

\mathrm{N}_{r}(M,\omega)=\mathcal{T}(M,[\omega])\lim_{q\rightarrow{\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{r}}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in H_{1}(M;\mathbb{Z})\\
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{b_{1}}\end{subarray}}C^{r}_{\omega,b,m}W_{\sigma(b\oplus\frac{mr}{\mathrm{GCD}(r,2)},s)},

where
C^{r}_{\omega,b,m}
are the same as in Theorem
4.25
.
Moreover, if the parts
(b)
of Conjectures
2.13
and
2.14
also hold and
F_{L}(x,q)
satisfies the assumption

, then one can take
W_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)=\widehat{Z}_{\mathfrak{s}}(q)
.

Example 4.27
.

Let
M
be obtained by surgery over a plumbing link corresponding to a “
Y

1

and

. So this provides an infinite family of examples in which Theorem

Note, the family of 3-manifolds in this example overlaps with families treated in connection with WRT invariants in
[
